In the Workers Protection Act, the rigid system of minimum working time for occupational physicians and safety workers is to be replaced by a risk-adapted, differentiated three-stage system. This will also effectively address the new risks in the working world, such as stress and psychosocial stress, in the workplace. These are new things, and one must also counteract these new things with new methods. Furthermore, under the new Labour Inspectorate Act, labour inspection will become a service and service facility for businesses and their employees within the framework of their legal tasks and possibilities. At the same time, the discretion of the labour inspectors to announce their controls – because it is not possible or very limited under applicable law – is to be significantly upgraded. In the case of minor infringements, the current obligation of labour inspection to conduct criminal proceedings in the event of a repetition should be waived and, at the same time, the penalties for certain minor infringements on structural measures and the like within certain tolerance limits should at all be avoided. So: "Consultation instead of punishment" goes through here. This is very positive, of course it is most positive in the minimum time spent. It is simply not to be seen that I am dealing with an office workplace just as it was before – like a job of a construction worker on the scaffolding stage – that is completely different risks – when I evaluate the medical and safety aspects. Therefore, from our point of view, the differentiation of the most important contribution, without harming the protection, is to be done here in practice: 1.2 hours for office, 1.5 hours for other workers and 1.7 hours for night work – essentially; it is a slightly different division. But it will ensure protection accordingly. I understand that the occupational physicians have no joy, nor with the fact that you can make 25 percent of the prevention time yourself now, but still: It is one of the measures to protect and, in general, the better practice, better resolution. Point Report of the Health Committee on the Government Template (770 of the Supplements): Federal Law on the Pharmacovigilance Fund for Austria (Content Cash Act 2002) (869 of the Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e17365	We're on our way to the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. The debate opens up to Mr Riepl. Voluntary speaking time limit: 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e17393	Mr President. Values Members of the Federal Government! Dear Members of the House! The federal law on the pharmaceutical salary register, which is under discussion today, replaces the existing salary fund law from 1959. For employees in public pharmacies, this law provides the basis for a secure existence, similar to that of public servants. The remuneration of the employees of the pharmacies is not paid by the service provider, but by the salary register, which in turn is fed by defined contributions and by a surcharge by the owners of pharmacies. The system is not new, it has been adapted and has proved successful so far. The new regulation of the law is also EU-wide. The law also includes a re-regulation of family allowances, but also a re-regulation of the working time accounting of the employees in the pharmacies. This law therefore lays down current and high labour and social standards, but also wage-based standards. As a social democrat and trade unionist, I expressly welcome this law. My group will therefore also agree to this bill. Ladies and gentlemen! However, what I would like to say at this point is the latent inactivity of this Federal Government to strive for the equivalence of social and labour law standards for all workers' groups. I would also like to justify this. For example, § 34 of this Act now lays down a clearly formulated legal right to a death toll to the extent of the three-fold monthly reference, which, in the event of the death of an employee, comes to the payment. This is in itself good and justified, but why only for the employees in the pharmacies? Why is there not such a legal claim for all workers in our country? The government parties have so far remained guilty of the answer to this question. Perhaps in the course of the debate today we will receive an answer. What I would like to suggest and encourage is not to abstain from legally standardised claims for certain categories of workers, but also to seek equality. Our group is willing to cooperate constructively.

If for those who can make such mistakes – I say, happen; anyone who works makes mistakes, too, that's quite clear – there is a corresponding checkout in which they pay, then this does not burden the budget, because this is a very clear insurance benefit due to a liability insurance for which this professional group has paid. That's why it's so incomprehensible to me that you connect these two things and say: We don't have any money for that! – We don’t need any money for this, we just need to be prepared to pay for liability insurance for those professional groups that are involved in it – in which case it is mainly doctors – in order to ensure that the injured patients receive adequate compensation. This is at the heart of the matter, and we must work on it! I ask you, Mr Pumberger, that you support this and not say a little something has been done here, and now we are putting on the rest cushion and are happy that we have done something, even if perhaps the next ten years nothing goes on in this area. It can't be!

Point First reading: Parliament's motion for a resolution (Doc. Johann Maierund comrades concerning a federal law amending the Plant Protection Act (497/A)
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19200	We get to the 14th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. President. — The next item is the joint debate on the following motions for resolutions: Maier. Voluntary speaking time limit: 4 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19227	Mr President. High house! My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! With this proposal we combine two objectives: On the one hand, we want the regulation of a sampling and revision plan to be legally guaranteed in this law as well as in the other legal instruments of agricultural property law. We think this is necessary because errors have occurred within the framework of the indirect federal administration – we all know that, not only because of the reports of the European Commission. In the Food Act, in the agricultural property law, there is no such sample and revision plan, which represents an assignment to the national leaders. In addition to the phytosanitary legislation, such a scheme should be laid down in the feed law, the seed law and so on. The second concern we attach to this request is that we want to create a protection provision for farmers and for dealers. According to Article 25a of the Food Law, the competent Federal Minister has to inform the public if harmful foods are placed on the market. This, ladies and gentlemen, is a legal obligation. Unfortunately, there is no such scheme in agricultural property law. Even if seeds are illegally placed on the market – the same applies to plant protection products – the competent Federal Minister does not have the opportunity to inform the public from the law. It is very specific about the farmers and the dealers. In an answer to a question, Federal Minister Molterer told me that he will examine this proposal. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen of the government parties! We invite you to agree to this request, because we think it is justified and, of course, we also want to comply with the European Union's control principle. The European Union's control principle is: from the field to the table. – It is necessary to vote on the control bodies and controls. Unfortunately, this is not the case in the agricultural property law, as opposed to food law. The phytosanitary law is one of the materials of the agricultural property right. We may invite you, on behalf of our group, if this request is discussed in the committee responsible, to accept this concern of farmers, traders and entrepreneurs, as well as consumers.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19281	The next speaker was Mr Zellot. Voluntary time limit. 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2001-11-23-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00085_d7e19303	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Mr Maier has already carried out the content of the request. It is, of course, quite understandable to me that you are concerned about consumers. However, I believe that agriculture and the practice of agriculture are very careful, especially in plant production with plant protection and with the provisions of the Plant Protection Act. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] This will be clear in connection with the various environmental programmes and, as we discussed yesterday, through the sharp, precise and efficient controls of the agricultural market in Austria. The Plant Protection Act also provides for monitoring by the Federal Office and the Research Centre for Agriculture. It also regulates what supervisory bodies are entitled to, it regulates the withdrawal of samples of plant protection products and the division of samples, and it also contains clear provisions in the event that a business or business owner refuses to tolerate an official act in this regard. However, this law also regulates the precise control, in particular customs surveillance, subsequent examination and verification of the samples and, finally, the notification to be returned to the competent administrative authority. I would like to mention this very consciously, so that it is also clear that Austrian agriculture, especially plant production in this case, is subject to very efficient and, of course, very sharp provisions by means of the plant protection legislation. However, I would also like to point out that consumer protection – also for you as the top consumer protection expert, Mr Maier – would often be better in future to make it clear to consumers that a very careful handling of plant protection products is taking place in the field of agricultural production, and then the food would also be better to sell!
